<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the entropy photon-number inequality for two independent bosonic inputs with finite mean energy, for every finite number of modes. This resolves the entropy photon-number conjecture in this finite-energy setting while allowing arbitrary entanglement among the modes within either input. As a consequence, we determine the exact minimum output entropy at fixed input entropy for every finite tensor power of an identical thermal attenuator, over finite-energy inputs that may be entangled across modes. We also obtain the exact classical capacity region of the degraded two-receiver pure-loss bosonic broadcast channel under a mean photon constraint.

- **claim-epni** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-epni)): Let $n\ge1$ be finite, and let $\rho_A,\rho_B$ be states on $n$ bosonic modes, with annihilation operators $a_1,\ldots,a_n$ and $b_1,\ldots,b_n$, respectively, satisfying $$\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho_A\sum_{j=1}^n a_j^\dagger a_j<\infty, \qquad \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho_B\sum_{j=1}^n b_j^\dagger b_j<\infty.$$ The joint input is $\rho_A\otimes\rho_B$; no independence assumption is made among the modes within either state. For $0\le\eta\le1$, let $\rho_C$ be the reduced state of the modes $$c_j=\sqrt\eta\,a_j+\sqrt{1-\eta}\,b_j,\qquad 1\le j\le n,$$ after passive beam-splitter mixing. Then $$\label{eq:epni} g^{-1}\!\left(\frac{S(\rho_C)}n\right) \ge \eta\,g^{-1}\!\left(\frac{S(\rho_A)}n\right) +(1-\eta)\,g^{-1}\!\left(\frac{S(\rho_B)}n\right),$$ where $S(\rho)=-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho\log\rho$.
  - Explanation: For two independent finite-energy inputs on any finite number of modes, mixed on a beam splitter, the inverse of the entropy function applied to the per-mode output entropy is at least the transmissivity-weighted average of the same quantity for the two inputs. Entanglement among the modes inside one input is allowed.
- **claim-thermal-attenuator-minimum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-thermal-attenuator)): Let $n\ge1$ be finite, let $s,N_B\ge0$ be finite, and let $0\le\eta\le1$. Every state $\rho$ on $n$ modes with $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho\sum_{j=1}^n a_j^\dagger a_j<\infty$ satisfies $$\label{eq:thermal-attenuator-bound} \frac1n S\!\left(\mathcal E_{\eta,N_B}^{\otimes n}(\rho)\right) \ge g\!\left(\eta g^{-1}\!\left(\frac{S(\rho)}n\right) +(1-\eta)N_B\right).$$ In particular, the exact constrained minimum is $$\label{eq:thermal-attenuator-minimum} \min_{\substack{\rho\text{ a state on }n\text{ modes}\\ \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho\sum_{j=1}^n a_j^\dagger a_j<\infty\\ S(\rho)=ns}} \frac1n S\!\left(\mathcal E_{\eta,N_B}^{\otimes n}(\rho)\right) =g\!\left(\eta g^{-1}(s)+(1-\eta)N_B\right).$$ It is attained by the product thermal input $\tau_{g^{-1}(s)}$. No independence assumption is imposed among the modes of $\rho$; only the environment is required to be independent of that input.
  - Explanation: For identical thermal attenuators acting on every mode, the output entropy per mode is bounded below by the entropy function evaluated at the mixed entropy photon number. The minimum at fixed input entropy is attained by a product thermal input, so entanglement across modes does not help.
- **claim-gibbs-variational** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-gibbs-variational)): Let $\sigma$ be a faithful state, and suppose both $S(\rho)$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho(-\log\sigma)$ are finite. Then $$D(\rho\Vert\sigma):=-S(\rho)+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho(-\log\sigma)\ge0,$$ with equality if and only if $\rho=\sigma$. Consequently, if $K$ is bounded below and $0<Z=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-K}<\infty$, its Gibbs state $Z^{-1}e^{-K}$ uniquely minimizes $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho K-S(\rho)$ among states for which these expressions are finite, provided that Gibbs state belongs to the admitted class.
  - Explanation: Relative entropy against a faithful state is nonnegative and vanishes only at that state. As a consequence the Gibbs state uniquely minimizes energy minus entropy within the admitted class.
- **claim-energy-compactness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-energy-compactness)): For each finite $E\ge0$, the set of states with $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho N\le E$ is compact in trace norm. On this set entropy is continuous and obeys $$\label{eq:entropy-energy} 0\le S(\rho)\le n g(E/n).$$ Every finite-energy state is a trace-norm limit of normalized finite-number truncations with uniformly bounded energies and convergent entropies.
  - Explanation: Energy sublevel sets of states are trace-norm compact, entropy is continuous on them and bounded by the thermal value, and finite-energy states are limits of finite-number truncations.
- **claim-thermal-mixing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-thermal-mixing)): Suppose independent ports carry the $n$-mode states $\tau_{r_s}$, and the retained modes are $\sum_s\sqrt{\lambda_s}\,d_{s,j}$, where $\lambda_s\ge0$ and $\sum_s\lambda_s=1$. Their joint retained state is $$\tau_{\sum_s\lambda_s r_s}.$$
  - Explanation: Passive mixing of independent product thermal states gives a product thermal state whose mean photon number is the weighted average of the input means.
- **claim-four-weight-interpolation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-interpolation)): At every coordinate of every space, let $m>0$ and $x,y\in\mathbb R$ be given. Suppose each $Z\in\mathcal T$ is supported on finitely many values of the parameter tuple $(m,x,y)$, and $\mathcal T$ is invariant under scalar multiplication by functions of this tuple. If the four families $$\label{int:four-weights} mf(x)e^x,\quad mf(x)e^{-x},\quad mf(y)e^y,\quad mf(y)e^{-y}$$ satisfy comparison, then so does $$\label{int:target-weight} F=m\frac{f(x)f(y)}{f(x+y)}.$$
  - Explanation: If four diagonal weight families built from the hyperbolic functions satisfy a form comparison, the composite target weight satisfies it too. No compatibility between the linear maps and the multipliers is needed.
- **claim-stieltjes-representation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-int-stieltjes)): The function $B$ extends holomorphically to the upper half-plane and across the positive real axis, is real and strictly decreasing there, and satisfies $B(1)=1$, $B'(1)=-1$. There are a constant $c\ge0$ and a positive measure $\nu$ on $[0,\infty)$ such that $$\label{int:stieltjes-form} -B'(s)=c+\int_{[0,\infty)}\frac{d\nu(r)}{s+r} \quad(s>0),\qquad \int_{[0,\infty)}\frac{d\nu(r)}{1+r}<\infty.$$ In particular $-B'(s)>0$ for every $s>0$.
  - Explanation: The implicitly defined function extends analytically, is strictly decreasing on the positive axis with prescribed value and slope at one, and its negative derivative is a Stieltjes transform plus a nonnegative constant, hence positive.
- **claim-gibbs-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-gibbs-regularity)): Let $W=1+N$ on the $n$-mode Fock space. Suppose $k>0$ and $B$ is a bounded positive operator. The positive form $$K=kW^4+W^{1/2}BW^{1/2},\qquad \mathcal D(K^{1/2})=\mathcal D(W^2),$$ defines a positive self-adjoint operator with compact inverse. Its normalized Gibbs state $\omega=e^{-K}/\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-K}$ is faithful and satisfies $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\omega W^7<\infty$. It uniquely minimizes $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\sigma K-S(\sigma)$ among states with finite fourth moment.
  - Explanation: With a fourth-power number penalty plus a bounded perturbation, the Gibbs state is faithful with finite seventh moment and uniquely minimizes the free-energy functional among finite fourth-moment states.
- **claim-regularized-minimum** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-regularized-minimum)): For every $0<\zeta\le\zeta_0$, the functional $J_\zeta$ attains a minimum of value at most $-c$. The input energies at these minima are bounded uniformly in $\zeta$. At each minimum all four states $\rho_s$, $s=0,1,2,D$, are faithful, have finite seventh moment, and satisfy $0\le H_s:=-\log\rho_s\le C_sW_s^4$ as forms. In fact $H_0\le CW_0$. The inputs obey the exact identities $$\label{eq:log-identity} \frac{(1+\varepsilon)w_i}{h_i}H_i =\frac{1-\kappa}{h_0}T_iH_0 +\varepsilon w_iN_i+\zeta W_i^4+c_iI, \qquad i=1,2,$$ where $T_i$ is the output-observable slice with the other two inputs fixed, and $c_i$ is a real scalar. The moment and logarithm bounds need not be uniform as $\zeta\downarrow0$.
  - Explanation: The regularized functional attains a negative minimum with uniformly bounded energies. The minimizers are faithful with regular logarithms and obey exact stationarity identities linking inputs and output.
- **claim-hessian-contraction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-hessian)): For $i=1,2$, the slice map on the centered output space satisfies $$\label{eq:hessian} \\|L_iZ\\|_i^2 \le\frac{1+\varepsilon}{1-\kappa}\,\\|Z\\|_0^2.$$ It consequently extends continuously to the completed centered spaces. For bounded observables this extension agrees with <a href="#eq:slice-map" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:slice-map">[eq:slice-map]</a>.
  - Explanation: At the minimizers the centered slice map contracts the logarithmic-mean metric from the output to each input, up to a fixed factor, and extends continuously to the completed spaces.
- **claim-interpolated-metric** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-metric)): The positive weights $$\label{eq:thermal-metric} F_s(v)=\frac1{h_s} \frac{f(h_sv/2)f(-h_s/2)}{f(h_s(v-1)/2)} =\frac{b_s(v)-(r_s+\tfrac12)v}{1-v}$$ satisfy <a href="#eq:weight-comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:weight-comparison">[eq:weight-comparison]</a>. The quotient at $v=1$ is understood continuously. For each fixed $r_s>0$ there are constants $0<c_s\le C_s<\infty$, independent of the minimizing states, such that $c_s\le F_s(v)\le C_s$ for every $v\in\mathbb R$.
  - Explanation: Applying the interpolation theorem yields a thermal-adapted weight that satisfies the comparison and is bounded above and below by constants independent of the minimizing states.
- **claim-dual-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-dual-finiteness)): At each minimizing state, $q_s$ has finite dual norm. Set $$K_{s,lr}=(r_s+1)p_r-r_sp_l.$$ Then $$\label{eq:dual-norm-sum} \\|q_s\\|_{F_s,*}^2 =\sum_{j,l,r}(1-v_s(l,r))K_{s,lr}\|(d'_{s,j})_{lr}\|^2.$$ The sums obtained by replacing the coefficient on the right by $\|K_{s,lr}\|$ or $\|v_s(l,r)K_{s,lr}\|$ are finite.
  - Explanation: The defect functionals have finite dual norm at each minimizer, given by an explicit logarithmic sum whose absolute versions also converge.
- **claim-weak-convolution** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-weak-traces)): Let $\mu_\gamma=\sum_i\sqrt{\lambda_i}\mu_i$ be the raw output mean. For every bounded output observable $Z$, $$\label{eq:defect-convolution} q_{0,j}(Z)=\sum_i w_iq_{i,j}(L_iZ) +\kappa(1-\kappa)\overline{(\mu_\gamma)_j} \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}[(\gamma-\tau_{r_0})Z].$$
  - Explanation: The output defect functional equals the weighted sum of input defects composed with the slice maps plus a correction from the replacement mixture and the output mean.
- **claim-defect-estimate** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-defect-estimate)): Set $$A_*^2=\sum_iw_i\\|q_i\\|_{F_i,*}^2, \qquad M_*^2=\sum_iw_i\|\mu_i\|^2, \qquad C_0=(h_0\inf_{v\in\mathbb R}F_0(v))^{-1/2}.$$ Then $$\label{eq:defect-triangle} \\|q_0\\|_{F_0,*}\le A_*+\sqrt{2\kappa}\,C_0M_*.$$ In particular, the previously reserved choice of $\kappa$ may be made small enough that $$\label{eq:defect-estimate} \\|q_0\\|_{F_0,*}^2 \le A_*^2+\frac\varepsilon2(A_*^2+M_*^2)$$ holds uniformly in $\zeta$ and the minimizing states.
  - Explanation: The output defect dual norm is controlled by the input defect norms and means, and for small enough replacement fraction the squared estimate holds with a small relative loss, uniformly in the minimizers.
- **claim-generator-covariance** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-generator-covariance)): At a minimizing pair, $\delta_s$ is traceless and $W_s^2\delta_sW_s^2$ is trace class. If $\Phi_i$ is the raw channel with the other two input states fixed, extended linearly to trace-class operators, then $$\label{eq:generator-covariance} \delta_0=(1-\kappa)\sum_i\Phi_i(\delta_i).$$ The traces against the forms $H_s$ and $W_s^4$ are well defined, and $$\label{eq:logarithmic-slicing} \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\delta_0H_0=(1-\kappa)\sum_i\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\delta_iT_iH_0.$$
  - Explanation: The relaxation expressions are traceless with trace-class weighted sandwiches, the output expression is the sliced combination of input expressions, and logarithmic pairings slice accordingly.
- **claim-entropy-production** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-entropy-production)): The quantities in <a href="#eq:stationarity" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:stationarity">[eq:stationarity]</a> satisfy $$\label{eq:entropy-production} \sigma_s=\\|q_s\\|_{F_s,*}^2-(e'_s-nr_s).$$
  - Explanation: The entropy-production pairing equals the squared dual norm of the defect functionals minus the centered-energy excess, which drives the exact cancellation in the stationarity identity.
- **claim-broadcast-capacity-region** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-broadcast-capacity)): Let $0\le\bar N<\infty$, $0\le\eta_C<\eta_B$, and $\eta_B+\eta_C\le1$. The classical capacity region for the channel and coding model above, with rates in nats per use, is the closed convex hull of the nonnegative pairs $(R_B,R_C)$ satisfying, for some $0\le\beta\le1$, $$\label{eq:broadcast-region} \begin{aligned} R_B&\le g(\eta_B\beta\bar N),\\ R_C&\le g(\eta_C\bar N)-g(\eta_C\beta\bar N), \end{aligned}$$ where $g$ is the natural-log function in <a href="#eq:g-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:g-definition">[eq:g-definition]</a>.
  - Explanation: The classical capacity region of the degraded two-receiver pure-loss broadcast channel under an averaged mean photon constraint is the closed convex hull of rate pairs given by the entropy function, with a converse from the vacuum-port inequality and achievability from coherent superposition coding.

{
  "claims": [
    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-epni",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge1$ be finite, and let $\\rho_A,\\rho_B$ be states on $n$ bosonic modes, with annihilation operators $a_1,\\ldots,a_n$ and $b_1,\\ldots,b_n$, respectively, satisfying $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho_A\\sum_{j=1}^n a_j^\\dagger a_j<\\infty,\n \\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho_B\\sum_{j=1}^n b_j^\\dagger b_j<\\infty.$$ The joint input is $\\rho_A\\otimes\\rho_B$; no independence assumption is made among the modes within either state. For $0\\le\\eta\\le1$, let $\\rho_C$ be the reduced state of the modes $$c_j=\\sqrt\\eta\\,a_j+\\sqrt{1-\\eta}\\,b_j,\\qquad 1\\le j\\le n,$$ after passive beam-splitter mixing. Then $$\\label{eq:epni}\n g^{-1}\\!\\left(\\frac{S(\\rho_C)}n\\right)\n \\ge \\eta\\,g^{-1}\\!\\left(\\frac{S(\\rho_A)}n\\right)\n +(1-\\eta)\\,g^{-1}\\!\\left(\\frac{S(\\rho_B)}n\\right),$$ where $S(\\rho)=-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho\\log\\rho$.",
        "source_id": "PM-273.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-thm-epni",
          "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge1$ be finite, and let $\\rho_A,\\rho_B$ be states on $n$ bosonic modes, with annihilation operators $a_1,\\ldots,a_n$ and $b_1,\\ldots,b_n$, respectively, satisfying $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho_A\\sum_{j=1}^n a_j^\\dagger a_j<\\infty,\n \\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho_B\\sum_{j=1}^n b_j^\\dagger b_j<\\infty.$$ The joint input is $\\rho_A\\otimes\\rho_B$; no independence assumption is made among the modes within either state. For $0\\le\\eta\\le1$, let $\\rho_C$ be the reduced state of the modes $$c_j=\\sqrt\\eta\\,a_j+\\sqrt{1-\\eta}\\,b_j,\\qquad 1\\le j\\le n,$$ after passive beam-splitter mixing. Then $$\\label{eq:epni}\n g^{-1}\\!\\left(\\frac{S(\\rho_C)}n\\right)\n \\ge \\eta\\,g^{-1}\\!\\left(\\frac{S(\\rho_A)}n\\right)\n +(1-\\eta)\\,g^{-1}\\!\\left(\\frac{S(\\rho_B)}n\\right),$$ where $S(\\rho)=-\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho\\log\\rho$.",
          "source_id": "PM-273.1"

      "exact_statement": "Let $n\\ge1$ be finite, and let $\\rho_A,\\rho_B$ be states on $n$ bosonic modes, with annihilation operators $a_1,\\ldots,a_n$ and $b_1,\\ldots,b_n$, respectively, satisfying $$\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho_A\\sum_{j=1}^n a_j^\\dagger a_j<\\infty,\n \\qquad\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho_B\\sum_{j=1}^n b_j^\\dagger b_j<\\infty.$$ The joint input is $\\rho_A\\otimes\\rho_B$; no independence assumption is made among the modes within either state.       "explanation": "For two independent finite-energy inputs on any finite number of modes, mixed on a beam splitter, the inverse of the entropy function applied to the per-mode output entropy is at least the transmissivity-weighted average of the same quantity for the two inputs.         "anchor": "stmt-cor-thermal-attenuator",
        "exact_quote": "Let $n\\ge1$ be finite, let $s,N_B\\ge0$ be finite, and let $0\\le\\eta\\le1$. Every state $\\rho$ on $n$ modes with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho\\sum_{j=1}^n a_j^\\dagger a_j<\\infty$ satisfies $$\\label{eq:thermal-attenuator-bound}\n \\frac1n S\\!\\left(\\mathcal E_{\\eta,N_B}^{\\otimes n}(\\rho)\\right)\n \\ge g\\!\\left(\\eta g^{-1}\\!\\left(\\frac{S(\\rho)}n\\right)\n                   +(1-\\eta)N_B\\right).$$ In particular, the exact constrained minimum is $$\\label{eq:thermal-attenuator-minimum}\n \\min_{\\substack{\\rho\\text{ a state on }n\\text{ modes}\\\\\n                  \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho\\sum_{j=1}^n a_j^\\dagger a_j<\\infty\\\\\n                  S(\\rho)=ns}}\n \\frac1n S\\!\\left(\\mathcal E_{\\eta,N_B}^{\\otimes n}(\\rho)\\right)\n =g\\!\\left(\\eta g^{-1}(s)+(1-\\eta)N_B\\right).$$ It is attained by the product thermal input $\\tau_{g^{-1}(s)}$. No independence assumption is imposed among the modes of $\\rho$; only the environment is required to be independent of that input.",
        "source_id": "PM-273.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-thermal-attenuator",

      "exact_statement": "Let $n\\ge1$ be finite, let $s,N_B\\ge0$ be finite, and let $0\\le\\eta\\le1$.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gibbs-variational",
        "exact_quote": "Let $\\sigma$ be a faithful state, and suppose both $S(\\rho)$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho(-\\log\\sigma)$ are finite. Then $$D(\\rho\\Vert\\sigma):=-S(\\rho)+\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho(-\\log\\sigma)\\ge0,$$ with equality if and only if $\\rho=\\sigma$. Consequently, if $K$ is bounded below and $0<Z=\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-K}<\\infty$, its Gibbs state $Z^{-1}e^{-K}$ uniquely minimizes $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho K-S(\\rho)$ among states for which these expressions are finite, provided that Gibbs state belongs to the admitted class.",
        "source_id": "PM-273.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-gibbs-variational",
          "exact_quote": "Let $\\sigma$ be a faithful state, and suppose both $S(\\rho)$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho(-\\log\\sigma)$ are finite.           "source_id": "PM-273.1"
        }

      "exact_statement": "Let $\\sigma$ be a faithful state, and suppose both $S(\\rho)$ and $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho(-\\log\\sigma)$ are finite.         "anchor": "stmt-lem-energy-compactness",
        "exact_quote": "For each finite $E\\ge0$, the set of states with $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\rho N\\le E$ is compact in trace norm.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-thm-interpolation",
        "exact_quote": "At every coordinate of every space, let $m>0$ and $x,y\\in\\mathbb R$ be given. Suppose each $Z\\in\\mathcal T$ is supported on finitely many values of the parameter tuple $(m,x,y)$, and $\\mathcal T$ is invariant under scalar multiplication by functions of this tuple. If the four families $$\\label{int:four-weights}\n mf(x)e^x,\\quad mf(x)e^{-x},\\quad mf(y)e^y,\\quad mf(y)e^{-y}$$ satisfy comparison, then so does $$\\label{int:target-weight}\n F=m\\frac{f(x)f(y)}{f(x+y)}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-273.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {

          "source_id": "PM-273.1"

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-int-stieltjes",
        "exact_quote": "The function $B$ extends holomorphically to the upper half-plane and across the positive real axis, is real and strictly decreasing there, and satisfies $B(1)=1$, $B'(1)=-1$. There are a constant $c\\ge0$ and a positive measure $\\nu$ on $[0,\\infty)$ such that $$\\label{int:stieltjes-form}\n -B'(s)=c+\\int_{[0,\\infty)}\\frac{d\\nu(r)}{s+r}\n \\quad(s>0),\\qquad\n \\int_{[0,\\infty)}\\frac{d\\nu(r)}{1+r}<\\infty.$$ In particular $-B'(s)>0$ for every $s>0$.",
        "source_id": "PM-273.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-int-stieltjes",
          "exact_quote": "The function $B$ extends holomorphically to the upper half-plane and across the positive real axis, is real and strictly decreasing there, and satisfies $B(1)=1$, $B'(1)=-1$.           "source_id": "PM-273.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "The function $B$ extends holomorphically to the upper half-plane and across the positive real axis, is real and strictly decreasing there, and satisfies $B(1)=1$, $B'(1)=-1$.       "explanation": "The implicitly defined function extends analytically, is strictly decreasing on the positive axis with prescribed value and slope at one, and its negative derivative is a Stieltjes transform plus a nonnegative constant, hence positive.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-gibbs-regularity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $W=1+N$ on the $n$-mode Fock space. Suppose $k>0$ and $B$ is a bounded positive operator. The positive form $$K=kW^4+W^{1/2}BW^{1/2},\\qquad \\mathcal D(K^{1/2})=\\mathcal D(W^2),$$ defines a positive self-adjoint operator with compact inverse. Its normalized Gibbs state $\\omega=e^{-K}/\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-K}$ is faithful and satisfies $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\omega W^7<\\infty$. It uniquely minimizes $\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\sigma K-S(\\sigma)$ among states with finite fourth moment.",

      },

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-prop-regularized-minimum",
        "exact_quote": "For every $0<\\zeta\\le\\zeta_0$, the functional $J_\\zeta$ attains a minimum of value at most $-c$. The input energies at these minima are bounded uniformly in $\\zeta$. At each minimum all four states $\\rho_s$, $s=0,1,2,D$, are faithful, have finite seventh moment, and satisfy $0\\le H_s:=-\\log\\rho_s\\le C_sW_s^4$ as forms. In fact $H_0\\le CW_0$.         "anchor": "stmt-prop-defect-estimate",
        "exact_quote": "Set $$A_*^2=\\sum_iw_i\\|q_i\\|_{F_i,*}^2,\n \\qquad M_*^2=\\sum_iw_i|\\mu_i|^2,\n \\qquad C_0=(h_0\\inf_{v\\in\\mathbb R}F_0(v))^{-1/2}.$$ Then $$\\label{eq:defect-triangle}\n \\|q_0\\|_{F_0,*}\\le A_*+\\sqrt{2\\kappa}\\,C_0M_*.$$ In particular, the previously reserved choice of $\\kappa$ may be made small enough that $$\\label{eq:defect-estimate}\n \\|q_0\\|_{F_0,*}^2\n \\le A_*^2+\\frac\\varepsilon2(A_*^2+M_*^2)$$ holds uniformly in $\\zeta$ and the minimizing states.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-lem-generator-covariance",
        "exact_quote": "At a minimizing pair, $\\delta_s$ is traceless and $W_s^2\\delta_sW_s^2$ is trace class. If $\\Phi_i$ is the raw channel with the other two input states fixed, extended linearly to trace-class operators, then $$\\label{eq:generator-covariance}\n \\delta_0=(1-\\kappa)\\sum_i\\Phi_i(\\delta_i).$$ The traces against the forms $H_s$ and $W_s^4$ are well defined, and $$\\label{eq:logarithmic-slicing}\n \\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\delta_0H_0=(1-\\kappa)\\sum_i\\mathop{\\mathrm{Tr}}\\delta_iT_iH_0.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-273.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-lem-generator-covariance",
          "exact_quote": "At a minimizing pair, $\\delta_s$ is traceless and $W_s^2\\delta_sW_s^2$ is trace class.           "source_id": "PM-273.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "At a minimizing pair, $\\delta_s$ is traceless and $W_s^2\\delta_sW_s^2$ is trace class.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-cor-broadcast-capacity",
        "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le\\bar N<\\infty$, $0\\le\\eta_C<\\eta_B$, and $\\eta_B+\\eta_C\\le1$. The classical capacity region for the channel and coding model above, with rates in nats per use, is the closed convex hull of the nonnegative pairs $(R_B,R_C)$ satisfying, for some $0\\le\\beta\\le1$, $$\\label{eq:broadcast-region}\n\\begin{aligned}\n R_B&\\le g(\\eta_B\\beta\\bar N),\\\\\n R_C&\\le g(\\eta_C\\bar N)-g(\\eta_C\\beta\\bar N),\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $g$ is the natural-log function in <a href=\"#eq:g-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:g-definition\">[eq:g-definition]</a>.",
        "source_id": "PM-273.1"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-cor-broadcast-capacity",
          "exact_quote": "Let $0\\le\\bar N<\\infty$, $0\\le\\eta_C<\\eta_B$, and $\\eta_B+\\eta_C\\le1$. The classical capacity region for the channel and coding model above, with rates in nats per use, is the closed convex hull of the nonnegative pairs $(R_B,R_C)$ satisfying, for some $0\\le\\beta\\le1$, $$\\label{eq:broadcast-region}\n\\begin{aligned}\n R_B&\\le g(\\eta_B\\beta\\bar N),\\\\\n R_C&\\le g(\\eta_C\\bar N)-g(\\eta_C\\beta\\bar N),\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $g$ is the natural-log function in <a href=\"#eq:g-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:g-definition\">[eq:g-definition]</a>.",
          "source_id": "PM-273.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $0\\le\\bar N<\\infty$, $0\\le\\eta_C<\\eta_B$, and $\\eta_B+\\eta_C\\le1$. The classical capacity region for the channel and coding model above, with rates in nats per use, is the closed convex hull of the nonnegative pairs $(R_B,R_C)$ satisfying, for some $0\\le\\beta\\le1$, $$\\label{eq:broadcast-region}\n\\begin{aligned}\n R_B&\\le g(\\eta_B\\beta\\bar N),\\\\\n R_C&\\le g(\\eta_C\\bar N)-g(\\eta_C\\beta\\bar N),\n\\end{aligned}$$ where $g$ is the natural-log function in <a href=\"#eq:g-definition\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"eq:g-definition\">[eq:g-definition]</a>.",
      "explanation": "The classical capacity region of the degraded two-receiver pure-loss broadcast channel under an averaged mean photon constraint is the closed convex hull of rate pairs given by the entropy function, with a converse from the vacuum-port inequality and achievability from coherent superposition coding.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **entropy photon number** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The entropy photon number of an $n$-mode bosonic state is the mean photon number per mode of the product of $n$ identical one-mode thermal states with the same total entropy.
- **entropy function g** ([source](#eq:g-definition)): Write $$\label{eq:g-definition} g(t)=(t+1)\log(t+1)-t\log t,\qquad t\ge0,$$ with $0\log0=0$. This continuous function increases from zero to infinity; its inverse is denoted by $g^{-1}$. All entropies and logarithms in this paper use the natural base.
- **multimode thermal state** ([source](#eq:thermal-definition)): For $r>0$, put $$\label{eq:thermal-definition} h(r)=\log(1+1/r),\qquad \tau_r=(r+1)^{-n}e^{-h(r)N}.$$ The product geometric law gives $$\label{eq:thermal-moments} \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\tau_rN=nr,\qquad S(\tau_r)=ng(r).$$ The state $\tau_r$ is faithful and has all number moments finite. At $r=0$, its limiting state is the multimode vacuum.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-gibbs-variational** [claim](#stmt-lem-gibbs-variational) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gibbs-variational): Jensen on the spectral measure plus the elementary inequality for p log(p/q) -> Nonnegativity and equality case of relative entropy; Gibbs minimizer
- **step-energy-compactness** [claim](#stmt-lem-energy-compactness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-energy-compactness): Comparison with geometric probabilities, number cutoff and pinching estimates -> Compactness, entropy continuity and thermal entropy bound on energy sublevel sets
- **step-thermal-mixing** [claim](#stmt-prop-thermal-mixing) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-thermal-mixing): Coherent-state Gaussian representation of thermal states and rotation of amplitudes -> Mixed thermal states are thermal with averaged mean
- **step-stieltjes** [claim](#stmt-int-stieltjes) / [proof](#proof-stmt-int-stieltjes): Conformal parametrization, analytic continuation and two Herglotz representations -> Stieltjes form of the negative derivative of B
- **step-interpolation** [claim](#stmt-thm-interpolation) / [proof](#pm-h-11): Parallel sums and logarithmic means preserve comparison; Stieltjes integral gives the extra weight; exact scalar minimization -> Comparison for the composite hyperbolic weight
- **step-gibbs-regularity** [claim](#stmt-lem-gibbs-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-gibbs-regularity): Closed form construction, compact inverse, eigenvalue counting and relative entropy -> Faithful Gibbs state with finite seventh moment as unique minimizer
- **step-regularized-minimum** [claim](#stmt-prop-regularized-minimum) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-regularized-minimum): Coercivity, compactness of a minimizing sequence, cross-entropy majorant and Gibbs identification -> Minimizers with logarithm identities and moment bounds
- **step-hessian** [claim](#stmt-prop-hessian) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-hessian): Second variation of relative entropy compared with a Gibbs dual lower bound -> Contraction of the slice map in the logarithmic-mean metric
- **step-metric** [claim](#stmt-prop-metric) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-metric): Apply the interpolation theorem to the four elementary comparisons -> Bounded thermal-adapted weight satisfying comparison
- **step-dual-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-lem-dual-finiteness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-dual-finiteness): Moment bounds on eigenvectors and representation of the functional in the weighted inner product -> Finite dual norm with explicit sum
- **step-weak-convolution** [claim](#stmt-lem-weak-traces) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-weak-traces): Weak commutator cancellation after rotation and centering of the replaced output -> Convolution identity for defect functionals
- **step-defect-estimate** [claim](#stmt-prop-defect-estimate) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-defect-estimate): Cauchy–Schwarz with the metric comparison, a variance bound for the mixture term and Young's inequality -> Uniform defect estimate
- **step-generator-covariance** [claim](#stmt-lem-generator-covariance) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-generator-covariance): Finite-cutoff covariance computation extended by weighted trace norm limits -> Covariance and logarithmic slicing of relaxation expressions
- **step-entropy-production** [claim](#stmt-lem-entropy-production) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-entropy-production): Eigenbasis evaluation of the dissipators and subtraction from the dual norm sum -> Entropy production identity
- **step-epni** [claim](#stmt-thm-epni) / [proof](#pm-h-22): Contradiction with a strict violation: regularized minimizer, exact cancellation, vanishing defects, thermal limit and entropy continuity -> The entropy photon-number inequality
- **step-thermal-attenuator** [claim](#stmt-cor-thermal-attenuator) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-thermal-attenuator): Apply the inequality with a thermal environment, then attain it with a product thermal input -> Exact constrained minimum output entropy
- **step-broadcast-capacity** [claim](#stmt-cor-broadcast-capacity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-broadcast-capacity): Converse via the vacuum-port inequality, Fano and Holevo bounds and convexity; achievability via coherent superposition coding with type control -> Capacity region of the degraded pure-loss broadcast channel

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-epni-thermal-comparison**: For two independent finite-energy inputs on a finite number of modes, with arbitrary entanglement allowed inside each input, passive mixing gives an output entropy that dominates the transmissivity-weighted combination of the two inputs' entropy photon numbers.
- **inv-thermal-attenuator-minimum**: At fixed input entropy, the minimum normalized output entropy of an identical thermal attenuator tensor power is attained by the product thermal input, even over inputs entangled across modes.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-infinite-energy-inputs**: Apply the entropy photon-number inequality to inputs of infinite mean photon number. -> The proof depends on compactness of energy sublevel sets and finite entropy; neither is available without finite energy.
- **anti-correlated-environment**: Use the constrained-minimum formula for a channel whose environment is correlated with the input or has mode-dependent parameters. -> The formula is derived only for an independent product thermal environment with identical parameters in every mode.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main results are inequalities and exact minima over all finite-energy states on an infinite-dimensional Fock space, proved by contradiction through a regularized variational minimum; no finite procedure computing a certified output is stated, so a finite oracle with planted mutants cannot decide them.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorems are existence and inequality statements proved by contradiction on infinite-dimensional Fock space. They give no executable specification. Their only closed forms are scalar expressions in the entropy function and its inverse. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No dynamics, sampling scheme or simulation step is specified. The paper reasons about exact quantum states and thermal relaxation generators only as analytic expressions at hypothetical minimizers. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper supplies no procedure that a user-facing tool could wrap. The only computable outputs are the capacity-region boundary and the minimum-output-entropy formula, both covered under algorithms. |
| algorithms | True | weak | adaptable | inferred | The corollary on the thermal attenuator gives a closed-form minimum, and the broadcast corollary gives a one-parameter family of boundary rate pairs. Both are finite scalar evaluations once the inverse of the entropy function is available. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No representation, index or update structure is proposed. States are treated as operators on an infinite-dimensional space, and finite truncations are only a proof device for approximation. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The passive mixing model is a physical idealization used in the statement, not a design for a device. The paper contains no noise budget, loss model beyond the ideal attenuator, or hardware specification. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No security notion, adversary or key is defined. The broadcast corollary concerns classical message rates, not secrecy, and the paper explicitly makes no wiretap claim. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no learning problem, data or training objective. Its regularized functionals are proof devices for a contradiction, not losses to optimize. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | A Gibbs variational principle and regularized minima appear, but only as steps of an existence argument by contradiction. The paper provides no solver, convergence rate or constructive minimization method. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The multi-receiver setting is an information-theoretic capacity model with no protocol, consensus or message-passing component. The receivers are explicitly non-cooperating and the model has no feedback. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Its natural home is quantum information theory and mathematical physics, which is not an engineering domain in this list. Its use is as a proved inequality that bounds what bosonic channels can achieve. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-finite-energy-finite-modes** (hypothesis): The entropy photon-number inequality is proved only for a finite number of modes and for inputs of finite mean energy. The energy-sublevel compactness and entropy continuity that the argument uses are not available outside this class. Blocked use: ['Applying the inequality to inputs with infinite mean photon number', 'Passing to a countably infinite number of modes without a separate argument']
- **limit-product-across-ports** (hypothesis): The joint input is a product across the two ports. Arbitrary correlation or entanglement is allowed among the modes inside either input, but the source gives no result when the two ports are correlated with each other. Blocked use: ['Using the inequality for inputs that are correlated or entangled between the two ports', 'Reading the no-independence remark as covering cross-port correlations']
- **limit-thermal-attenuator-environment** (formal_scope): The constrained minimum is stated for identical attenuators with an independent product thermal environment. Only the environment is required to be independent of the input, and the corollary asserts attainment by the thermal input. Blocked use: ['Using the formula for mode-dependent attenuations', 'Using the formula for an environment correlated with the input', 'Treating the thermal input as the unique minimizer']
- **limit-proof-by-contradiction** (nonconstructive): The main inequality is obtained by contradiction from a hypothetical strict violation. The regularized minimizers come from compactness, so the source gives no finite procedure that computes them or certifies the inequality on a given pair of states. Blocked use: ['Using the proof as an algorithm to find extremal states', 'Using the argument as a numerical certifier of the inequality on sampled states']
- **limit-nonuniform-regularization** (approximation): At the regularized minima the moment and logarithm bounds are not uniform as the fourth-moment penalty parameter goes to zero. Only the energy bounds are uniform, so no convergence of higher moments can be inferred. Blocked use: ['Assuming uniform higher-moment control in the limit of vanishing penalty', 'Quoting the minimizer bounds as constants independent of the penalty']
- **limit-gibbs-admitted-class** (hypothesis): The Gibbs minimizer property holds only when the Gibbs state itself belongs to the admitted class and the relevant expressions are finite. Blocked use: ['Invoking the Gibbs variational principle when the Gibbs state lies outside the admitted class', 'Using it when the entropy or the energy expectation is infinite']
- **limit-interpolation-finite-parameters** (hypothesis): The interpolation theorem needs source tests supported on finitely many values of the parameter tuple, and a test space invariant under multiplication by functions of that tuple. Without these conditions the composite-weight comparison is not established. Blocked use: ['Applying the four-weight interpolation to test spaces with infinitely many parameter values', 'Applying it to test spaces that are not closed under parameter multipliers']
- **limit-broadcast-averaged-constraint** (formal_scope): The broadcast region is for the stated coding model. The photon constraint is averaged over uses and the whole codebook, and the receivers neither cooperate nor use feedback. Blocked use: ['Applying the region under a per-codeword photon constraint', 'Applying it with receiver cooperation or feedback']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 273 (`lean/docs/273.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The entropy photon-number inequality](../../preprints/The-entropy-photon-number-inequality-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization proves the entropy photon-number inequality for two independent bosonic inputs with finite mean energy and any finite positive number $n$ of modes. Write $N(\rho)=g^{-1}(S(\rho)/n)$, where $S$ is von Neumann entropy and $g(x)=(x+1)\log(x+1)-x\log x$ is the thermal entropy per mode. For a beam splitter of transmissivity $0\le\eta\le1$, its output satisfies $N(\rho_C)\ge\eta N(\rho_A)+(1-\eta)N(\rho_B)$.
>
> Entanglement among modes within either input is allowed. The thermal-attenuator minimum-output-entropy and broadcast-capacity consequences in the paper are outside this selected inequality.

Comparator source `EntropyPhotonNumber.lean` (4818 bytes, sha256 `852aaa9ae3c137dcbe311da89c8467f09bb25b592c2ebb79a3d9cd079b2dd016`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

noncomputable section

open scoped BigOperators ComplexConjugate

namespace EntropyPhotonNumber

/-- The number-basis indices for `n` bosonic modes. -/
abbrev NumberIndex (n : ℕ) := Fin n → ℕ

/-- The full, untruncated bosonic Fock space `ℓ²(ℕⁿ, ℂ)`. -/
abbrev Fock (n : ℕ) := lp (fun _ : NumberIndex n => ℂ) 2

/-- A number-basis vector. -/
def numberKet {n : ℕ} (k : NumberIndex n) : Fock n := lp.single 2 k 1

/-- Matrix entries of an operator in the number basis. -/
def entry {n : ℕ} (T : Fock n →L[ℂ] Fock n) (k l : NumberIndex n) : ℂ :=
  inner ℂ (numberKet k) (T (numberKet l))

/-- Positive trace-one operators. For a positive bounded operator on this
Hilbert space, summability of its diagonal is the positive trace-class
criterion; `HasSum ... 1` includes summability as well as normalization. -/
structure State (n : ℕ) where
  op : Fock n →L[ℂ] Fock n
  positive : op.IsPositive
  trace_one : HasSum (fun k => (entry op k k).re) 1

/-- Total number of quanta in a number-basis vector. -/
def totalNumber {n : ℕ} (k : NumberIndex n) : ℕ := ∑ j, k j

/-- Exactly finiteness of the expectation of the nonnegative number operator. -/
def FiniteEnergy {n : ℕ} (ρ : State n) : Prop :=
  Summable (fun k => (totalNumber k : ℝ) * (entry ρ.op k k).re)

/-- `−Tr ρ log ρ`, using the continuous extension of `−t log t` at zero
and continuous functional calculus for the positive operator `ρ`. -/
def entropy {n : ℕ} (ρ : State n) : ℝ :=
  ∑' k, (entry (cfc (fun z : ℂ => (-(z.re * Real.log z.re) : ℂ)) ρ.op) k k).re

/-- The thermal entropy per mode, with the convention `0 log 0 = 0`. -/
def g (t : ℝ) : ℝ := (t + 1) * Real.log (t + 1) - t * Real.log t

/-- The inverse of increasing `g : [0,∞) → [0,∞)`, expressed by its lower
level cut. Only its values on `[0,∞)` occur in the main theorem. -/
def gInv (s : ℝ) : ℝ := sInf {t : ℝ | 0 ≤ t ∧ s ≤ g t}

/-- One-mode passive beam-splitter coefficient, in number bases. It is the
coefficient of `(c†)^k (d†)^e` in
`(√η c† − √(1−η) d†)^a (√(1−η) c† + √η d†)^b`,
with the normalized number-vector factorials. -/
def oneModeBeamCoefficient (η : ℝ) (k e a b : ℕ) : ℝ :=
  if k + e = a + b then
    Real.sqrt (((k.factorial : ℝ) * e.factorial) / (a.factorial * b.factorial)) *
      ∑ p ∈ Finset.range (a + 1), ∑ q ∈ Finset.range (b + 1),
        if p + q = k then
          (a.choose p : ℝ) * b.choose q *
            Real.sqrt η ^ p * (-Real.sqrt (1 - η)) ^ (a - p) *
            Real.sqrt (1 - η) ^ q * Real.sqrt η ^ (b - q)
        else 0
  else 0

/-- The same passive rotation is performed on all mode indices. -/
def beamCoefficient {n : ℕ} (η : ℝ) (k e a b : NumberIndex n) : ℝ :=
  ∏ j, oneModeBeamCoefficient η (k j) (e j) (a j) (b j)

/-- Choices of the first input number in the total-number sector of `(k,e)`. -/
abbrev SectorSplit {n : ℕ} (k e : NumberIndex n) := ∀ j, Fin (k j + e j + 1)

def splitLeft {n : ℕ} {k e : NumberIndex n} (a : SectorSplit k e) : NumberIndex n :=
  fun j => a j

def splitRight {n : ℕ} {k e : NumberIndex n} (a : SectorSplit k e) : NumberIndex n :=
  fun j => k j + e j - a j

/-- A single discarded-number diagonal block of `U(ρA ⊗ ρB)U†`.
The sums are finite because passive mixing preserves number in each paired
mode. The two unrestricted density matrices allow all internal entanglement. -/
def outputBlock {n : ℕ} (η : ℝ) (ρA ρB : State n)
    (k l e : NumberIndex n) : ℂ :=
  ∑ a : SectorSplit k e, ∑ b : SectorSplit l e,
    (beamCoefficient η k e (splitLeft a) (splitRight a) : ℂ) *
      entry ρA.op (splitLeft a) (splitLeft b) *
      entry ρB.op (splitRight a) (splitRight b) *
      (beamCoefficient η l e (splitLeft b) (splitRight b) : ℂ)

/-- Reduced state after passive beam-splitter mixing of independent ports.
The second row of the orthogonal rotation is `(-√(1−η),√η)`; changing its
sign has no effect on this reduced state. `HasSum` records the actual partial
trace, not a default value for a possibly divergent series. -/
def IsBeamSplitterOutput {n : ℕ} (η : ℝ) (ρA ρB ρC : State n) : Prop :=
  ∀ k l, HasSum (outputBlock η ρA ρB k l) (entry ρC.op k l)

/-- The entropy photon-number inequality for arbitrary finite-energy inputs
and any positive finite number of modes. -/
theorem entropy_photon_number_inequality
    (n : ℕ) (hn : 1 ≤ n) (ρA ρB ρC : State n)
    (hA : FiniteEnergy ρA) (hB : FiniteEnergy ρB)
    (η : ℝ) (hη : η ∈ Set.Icc 0 1)
    (hC : IsBeamSplitterOutput η ρA ρB ρC) :
    η * gInv (entropy ρA / n) + (1 - η) * gInv (entropy ρB / n) ≤
      gInv (entropy ρC / n) := by
  sorry

end EntropyPhotonNumber

end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the entropy photon-number inequality for two independent bosonic inputs with finite mean energy, for every finite number of modes. This resolves the entropy photon-number conjecture in this finite-energy setting while allowing arbitrary entanglement among the modes within either input. As a consequence, we determine the exact minimum output entropy at fixed input entropy for every finite tensor power of an identical thermal attenuator, over finite-energy inputs that may be entangled across modes. We also obtain the exact classical capacity region of the degraded two-receiver pure-loss bosonic broadcast channel under a mean photon constraint.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

The entropy photon number of an $n$-mode bosonic state is the mean photon number per mode of the product of $n$ identical one-mode thermal states with the same total entropy. Guha, Erkmen, and Shapiro conjectured that this quantity obeys a concavity inequality under beam-splitter mixing of independent inputs <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[GES2007](#ref-GES2007)]. Their entropy photon-number inequality is a quantum counterpart of the classical entropy power inequality and has consequences for minimum output entropy and the information capacities of bosonic channels. We prove its two-input form for finite-energy states.

<a id="eq:g-definition"></a>
Write $$\label{eq:g-definition}
 g(t)=(t+1)\log(t+1)-t\log t,\qquad t\ge0,$$ with $0\log0=0$. This continuous function increases from zero to infinity; its inverse is denoted by $g^{-1}$. All entropies and logarithms in this paper use the natural base.

<a id="stmt-thm-epni"></a>
<div id="thm:epni" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1** (Entropy photon-number inequality). *Let $n\ge1$ be finite, and let $\rho_A,\rho_B$ be states on $n$ bosonic modes, with annihilation operators $a_1,\ldots,a_n$ and $b_1,\ldots,b_n$, respectively, satisfying $$\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho_A\sum_{j=1}^n a_j^\dagger a_j<\infty,
 \qquad
 \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho_B\sum_{j=1}^n b_j^\dagger b_j<\infty.$$ The joint input is $\rho_A\otimes\rho_B$; no independence assumption is made among the modes within either state. For $0\le\eta\le1$, let $\rho_C$ be the reduced state of the modes $$c_j=\sqrt\eta\,a_j+\sqrt{1-\eta}\,b_j,\qquad 1\le j\le n,$$ after passive beam-splitter mixing. Then <a id="eq:epni"></a>$$\label{eq:epni}
 g^{-1}\!\left(\frac{S(\rho_C)}n\right)
 \ge \eta\,g^{-1}\!\left(\frac{S(\rho_A)}n\right)
 +(1-\eta)\,g^{-1}\!\left(\frac{S(\rho_B)}n\right),$$ where $S(\rho)=-\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho\log\rho$.*

</div>

Thus Theorem <a href="#thm:epni" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:epni">1</a> resolves the entropy photon-number conjecture positively for finite-energy inputs, including arbitrary internal multimode entanglement. Thermal inputs with a common mean photon number within each port give equality, even when the means at the two ports differ.

For the channel consequences, write $\tau_r$ for the $n$-mode product thermal state with mean photon number $r$ in each mode, including the vacuum at $r=0$; thus $S(\tau_r)=ng(r)$. Let $\mathcal E_{\eta,N_B}$ be the one-mode thermal attenuator obtained by retaining $c=\sqrt\eta\,a+\sqrt{1-\eta}\,b$ when the environment mode $b$ is thermal with mean photon number $N_B$ and is independent of the input. Its tensor power $\mathcal E_{\eta,N_B}^{\otimes n}$ uses the same parameters in every mode and the joint input $\rho\otimes\tau_{N_B}$.

<a id="stmt-cor-thermal-attenuator"></a>
<div id="cor:thermal-attenuator" class="corollary">

<a id="eq:thermal-attenuator-bound"></a>
<a id="eq:thermal-attenuator-minimum"></a>
**Corollary 2** (Constrained entropy minimum for identical thermal attenuators). *Let $n\ge1$ be finite, let $s,N_B\ge0$ be finite, and let $0\le\eta\le1$. Every state $\rho$ on $n$ modes with $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho\sum_{j=1}^n a_j^\dagger a_j<\infty$ satisfies $$\label{eq:thermal-attenuator-bound}
 \frac1n S\!\left(\mathcal E_{\eta,N_B}^{\otimes n}(\rho)\right)
 \ge g\!\left(\eta g^{-1}\!\left(\frac{S(\rho)}n\right)
                   +(1-\eta)N_B\right).$$ In particular, the exact constrained minimum is $$\label{eq:thermal-attenuator-minimum}
 \min_{\substack{\rho\text{ a state on }n\text{ modes}\\
                  \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho\sum_{j=1}^n a_j^\dagger a_j<\infty\\
                  S(\rho)=ns}}
 \frac1n S\!\left(\mathcal E_{\eta,N_B}^{\otimes n}(\rho)\right)
 =g\!\left(\eta g^{-1}(s)+(1-\eta)N_B\right).$$ It is attained by the product thermal input $\tau_{g^{-1}(s)}$. No independence assumption is imposed among the modes of $\rho$; only the environment is required to be independent of that input.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-cor-thermal-attenuator"><summary>Proof of stmt-cor-thermal-attenuator</summary>

*Proof.* The environment $\tau_{N_B}$ has finite total mean photon number $nN_B$ and entropy $ng(N_B)$. The retained output has finite energy because passive mixing preserves combined total number, so its entropy is finite by Lemma <a href="#lem:energy-compactness" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:energy-compactness">4</a>. Apply Theorem <a href="#thm:epni" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:epni">1</a> to the independent inputs $\rho$ and $\tau_{N_B}$, and then apply the increasing function $g$, to obtain <a href="#eq:thermal-attenuator-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:thermal-attenuator-bound">[eq:thermal-attenuator-bound]</a>.

For $r=g^{-1}(s)<\infty$, the state $\tau_r$ has finite total mean photon number $nr$ and entropy $ns$. Proposition <a href="#prop:thermal-mixing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:thermal-mixing">5</a> gives $$\mathcal E_{\eta,N_B}^{\otimes n}(\tau_r)
 =\tau_{\eta r+(1-\eta)N_B},$$ whose entropy is $n g(\eta r+(1-\eta)N_B)$. This attains the lower bound and proves <a href="#eq:thermal-attenuator-minimum" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:thermal-attenuator-minimum">[eq:thermal-attenuator-minimum]</a>. The endpoints are included: at $\eta=0$ the retained state is the environment, and at $\eta=1$ it is the input. When $s=0$ or $N_B=0$, the corresponding thermal state is the vacuum, covered by the zero-mean case of Proposition <a href="#prop:thermal-mixing" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:thermal-mixing">5</a>. ◻

</details>

Corollary <a href="#cor:thermal-attenuator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:thermal-attenuator">2</a> concerns identical passive thermal attenuators with an independent product thermal environment. It asserts attainment, not uniqueness, and makes no claim for mode-dependent attenuations, amplifiers, or additive-noise channels.

The vacuum case also determines the classical capacity region of a degraded pure-loss broadcast channel. Here one input mode per use is passively split with auxiliary vacuum modes independent of the input between receivers $B,C$ and an inaccessible loss output. The power transmissivities satisfy $0\le\eta_C<\eta_B$ and $\eta_B+\eta_C\le1$, so $C$’s marginal is obtained from $B$’s by a further pure-loss attenuation. Section <a href="#sec:broadcast" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:broadcast">7</a> gives the exact region for independent classical messages and separate collective decoding, without feedback or receiver cooperation. Its finite mean photon constraint is averaged over the codebook and uses, and its finite-energy codewords may be entangled across uses. The vacuum-port specialization of Theorem <a href="#thm:epni" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:epni">1</a> supplies the entropy input assumed in the converse of Guha, Shapiro, and Erkmen <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[GSE2007](#ref-GSE2007)]; coherent-state superposition coding supplies achievability.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Context and earlier results

Shannon introduced entropy power in his theory of communication <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[Shannon1948](#ref-Shannon1948)]; the Fisher-information proofs of Stam and Blachman <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[Stam1959](#ref-Stam1959), [Blachman1965](#ref-Blachman1965)] became central to its later development. For independent classical random vectors, the entropy power inequality compares the entropy of a sum with the entropies of its summands. Its bosonic counterpart replaces addition by passive mixing of quantum modes. The analogy is especially natural for thermal states: their mean photon numbers add with the beam-splitter weights, and their entropies are given by the same function $g$ appearing in Theorem <a href="#thm:epni" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:epni">1</a>. The formulation of Guha, Erkmen, and Shapiro <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[GES2007](#ref-GES2007), Section II.B] makes this thermal comparison the central conjecture and explains its consequences for bosonic communication. Earlier work had established the capacity of the pure-loss bosonic channel <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[GGLMSY2004](#ref-GGLMSY2004)] and connected noisy-channel capacity questions with minimum output entropy <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[GGLMS2004](#ref-GGLMS2004)]. The constrained minimum-output problem also appears in the bosonic broadcast-channel analysis of Guha, Shapiro, and Erkmen <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[GSE2007](#ref-GSE2007)]. Early EPnI special cases included one-mode number-diagonal inputs with two nonzero probabilities and a vacuum second port <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[DSM2011](#ref-DSM2011)].

<a id="eq:exponential-qepi"></a>
König and Smith <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[KS2014](#ref-KS2014)] established a quantum entropy power inequality using quantum Fisher information and a heat evolution. Their results include the linear bound $$S(\rho_C)\ge\eta S(\rho_A)+(1-\eta)S(\rho_B)$$ for every transmissivity, and the exponential entropy power bound for a balanced beam splitter. De Palma, Mari, and Giovannetti <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[DMG2014](#ref-DMG2014)] proved the exponential bound for every transmissivity, $$\label{eq:exponential-qepi}
 e^{S(\rho_C)/n}
 \ge\eta e^{S(\rho_A)/n}+(1-\eta)e^{S(\rho_B)/n}.$$ These results are formulated for an arbitrary finite number of modes and independent inputs, with no product assumption within a group. De Palma, Mari, Lloyd, and Giovannetti <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[DMLG2015](#ref-DMLG2015)] subsequently treated more general linear mixing of several independent groups of modes. De Palma and Trevisan later gave a rigorous finite-energy treatment of the Fisher-information and heat-flow steps using an integral formulation; their conditional inequality with a trivial memory system yields <a href="#eq:exponential-qepi" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exponential-qepi">[eq:exponential-qepi]</a> for arbitrary independent finite-energy inputs <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[DePalmaTrevisan2018](#ref-DePalmaTrevisan2018), Theorem 6].

The entropy photon-number inequality requires a sharper comparison than <a href="#eq:exponential-qepi" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:exponential-qepi">[eq:exponential-qepi]</a> when the input entropy levels differ. When $S(\rho_A)=S(\rho_B)$, the linear entropy bound already gives the EPnI value by monotonicity of $g^{-1}$. For unequal entropy levels, thermal inputs attain EPnI, whereas the exponential bound does not recover their exact output entropy. This distinction matters for the minimum output entropy problem with an input-entropy constraint: the bound must reproduce the exact thermal comparison at unequal entropy levels.

The EPnI itself was also known for two independent Gaussian inputs in any finite number of modes, including Gaussian correlations within either input; De Palma proves this case using symplectic eigenvalue comparisons <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[DePalmaThesis2017](#ref-DePalmaThesis2017), Appendix A.8].

The Gaussian optimizer theorem of Giovannetti, Holevo, and García-Patrón <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[GHG2015](#ref-GHG2015)] settled the unconstrained minimum-output problem for broad classes of Gaussian channels. Fixing a positive input entropy is a different optimization problem. For one-mode gauge-covariant channels, De Palma, Trevisan, and Giovannetti established optimality of passive rearrangement at fixed spectrum <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[DTG2016passive](#ref-DTG2016passive)], then the sharp entropy bound for the quantum-limited attenuator <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[DTG2017attenuator](#ref-DTG2017attenuator)], and finally the sharp bound for one-mode phase-covariant Gaussian channels with thermal noise <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[DTG2017](#ref-DTG2017), Theorem 4]. Their multimode extension <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[DTG2017multimode](#ref-DTG2017multimode), Theorem 11] covers inputs diagonal in some product basis, including classical correlations in that basis.

Later advances address further parts of this landscape. De Palma <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[DP2019](#ref-DP2019)] proved the sharp constrained bound for multimode entanglement-breaking Gaussian attenuators and amplifiers with thermal environments, and improved lower bounds for other parameter regimes. Beigi and Rahimi-Keshari <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[BeigiRahimiKeshari2025](#ref-BeigiRahimiKeshari2025)] proved a one-mode meta-logarithmic-Sobolev inequality that also gives a variational proof of the one-mode constrained minimum-output result. Beigi and Mehrabi <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[BeigiMehrabi2026](#ref-BeigiMehrabi2026)] established subset-convolution inequalities for exponential entropy power and entropy monotonicity under repeated symmetric quantum convolution of an identical input.

Theorem <a href="#thm:epni" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:epni">1</a> supplies the thermal comparison for two freely varying inputs, including arbitrary entanglement within either port. Corollary <a href="#cor:thermal-attenuator" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="cor:thermal-attenuator">2</a> specializes it to the exact finite-mode constrained minimum for identical thermal attenuators, without a product or separability restriction on the finite-energy input. Thermal states enter the proof of Theorem <a href="#thm:epni" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:epni">1</a> as reference states and as limits of regularized minimizers. The original inputs need not be Gaussian, number-diagonal, or independent across modes.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### Proof strategy

The difficulty is to compare the two freely varying inputs with thermal states at their respective entropy levels. We fix those thermal reference parameters near a hypothetical strictly violating pair. This turns the contradiction into a negative minimum of a linear combination of three entropies, after adding penalties that ensure compactness and regularity. The reference parameters remain fixed throughout the variational argument.

An independent analytic ingredient is an interpolation theorem for positive diagonal quadratic forms, proved in Section <a href="#sec:interpolation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:interpolation">3</a>. For positive scalars $m$ and real parameters $x,y$, it transforms four comparisons with weights $$mf(x)e^{x},\quad mf(x)e^{-x},\quad
 mf(y)e^{y},\quad mf(y)e^{-y},
 \qquad f(x)=\frac{x}{\sinh x},\quad f(0)=1,$$ into the comparison with weight $mf(x)f(y)/f(x+y)$. No compatibility is required between the linear maps and the diagonal multipliers. A Stieltjes representation for the derivative of an implicitly defined function is the essential analytic step. The theorem is formulated for finitely many spectral parameter values on source tests and permits unrestricted nonnegative target sums, so it also applies to the infinite matrix spaces used below.

Section <a href="#sec:minimization" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:minimization">4</a> develops the variational argument. Assuming a strict violation, we add a thermal port, a small thermal replacement of the output, a relative-entropy penalty, and a fourth-moment penalty. The resulting functional has faithful Gibbs minimizers with enough moment control for all subsequent traces. Varying one input at a time yields a component contraction in the logarithmic-mean metric *at those minimizers*. It is not asserted as a universal channel inequality.

Section <a href="#sec:metric" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:metric">5</a> combines two product-port variance estimates with the component contractions. These supply precisely the four hypotheses of the interpolation theorem. Its conclusion controls the norm of a centered thermal-balance defect: a linear functional measuring the creation–annihilation balance after subtracting the state’s mean. In Section <a href="#sec:stationarity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:stationarity">6</a>, pairing the Gibbs identities with the relaxation generators gives an exact cancellation of the centered energy terms. As the fourth-moment penalty vanishes, the remaining defects and means tend to zero; their number-basis identities force both limiting inputs to be thermal. Entropy continuity then contradicts the original strictly negative objective value.

The auxiliary regularizations have linked roles: the relative-entropy penalty gives energy coercivity and supplies the strict margin in the final defect and mean estimate, while the thermal port supplies the slack needed for the associated Hessian comparisons. Output replacement controls the output logarithm, and the moment penalty justifies the unbounded-generator calculations. Only the last penalty tends to zero in the concluding compactness argument. This avoids assuming differentiability of entropy along an unbounded evolution or convergence of the input energies.

Section <a href="#sec:preliminaries" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:preliminaries">2</a> supplies the entropy, compactness, and thermal-mixing facts used in this argument. Each of the specialized interpolation, minimization, and limiting statements is proved within the paper.

<a id="sec:preliminaries"></a>
### Bosonic states, energy, and entropy

We work on the $n$-mode Fock space $\mathcal H_n=\ell^2(\mathbb N_0^n)$, with number basis $\{|\mathbf k\rangle:\mathbf k\in\mathbb N_0^n\}$. For the annihilation operators $d_j$, $$d_j|\mathbf k\rangle=\sqrt{k_j}\,|\mathbf k-\mathbf e_j\rangle,
 \qquad N=\sum_{j=1}^n d_j^\dagger d_j,
 \qquad W=I+N.$$ A state is a positive trace-class operator of trace one. Expectations of positive unbounded operators are understood as increasing limits of bounded spectral truncations. Form inequalities have their usual quadratic-form meaning on the indicated domains.

<a id="eq:thermal-definition"></a>
For $r>0$, put $$\label{eq:thermal-definition}
 h(r)=\log(1+1/r),\qquad
 \tau_r=(r+1)^{-n}e^{-h(r)N}.$$ The product geometric law gives <a id="eq:thermal-moments"></a>$$\label{eq:thermal-moments}
 \mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\tau_rN=nr,\qquad S(\tau_r)=ng(r).$$ The state $\tau_r$ is faithful and has all number moments finite. At $r=0$, its limiting state is the multimode vacuum.

<a id="pm-h-6"></a>
#### Entropy estimates and compactness

We first record the entropy facts needed in the infinite-dimensional argument. The relative entropy introduced by Umegaki <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[Umegaki1962](#ref-Umegaki1962)] gives the Gibbs variational principle. For oscillator energy bounds, the compactness and entropy-continuity statements below are special cases of the general energy-constrained theory <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[Shirokov2006](#ref-Shirokov2006), [Winter2016](#ref-Winter2016)]; we include the elementary cutoff proof used here.

<a id="stmt-lem-gibbs-variational"></a>
<div id="lem:gibbs-variational" class="lemma">

**Lemma 3** (Relative entropy and the Gibbs variational inequality). *Let $\sigma$ be a faithful state, and suppose both $S(\rho)$ and $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho(-\log\sigma)$ are finite. Then $$D(\rho\Vert\sigma):=-S(\rho)+\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho(-\log\sigma)\ge0,$$ with equality if and only if $\rho=\sigma$. Consequently, if $K$ is bounded below and $0<Z=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}e^{-K}<\infty$, its Gibbs state $Z^{-1}e^{-K}$ uniquely minimizes $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho K-S(\rho)$ among states for which these expressions are finite, provided that Gibbs state belongs to the admitted class.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-gibbs-variational"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-gibbs-variational</summary>

*Proof.* Choose eigenvectors $\psi_j$ of $\rho$ with positive eigenvalues $p_j$, and set $q_j=\langle\psi_j,\sigma\psi_j\rangle$. Scalar Jensen applied to the spectral measure of $\sigma$ gives $$-\langle\psi_j,\log\sigma\,\psi_j\rangle\ge-\log q_j.$$ The elementary inequality $p\log(p/q)\ge p-q$ and $\sum_jq_j\le1$ now imply nonnegativity. Equality requires $p_j=q_j$, no mass of $\sigma$ outside the support of $\rho$, and equality in each Jensen inequality. The last condition makes each $\psi_j$ an eigenvector of $\sigma$ with eigenvalue $q_j$, so $\rho=\sigma$. The Gibbs assertion follows by substituting $-\log\sigma=K+\log Z$. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-lem-energy-compactness"></a>
<div id="lem:energy-compactness" class="lemma">

**Lemma 4** (Energy bound and compactness). *For each finite $E\ge0$, the set of states with $\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho N\le E$ is compact in trace norm. On this set entropy is continuous and obeys <a id="eq:entropy-energy"></a>$$\label{eq:entropy-energy}
 0\le S(\rho)\le n g(E/n).$$ Every finite-energy state is a trace-norm limit of normalized finite-number truncations with uniformly bounded energies and convergent entropies.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-energy-compactness"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-energy-compactness</summary>

*Proof.* Diagonal entropy in any orthonormal basis bounds von Neumann entropy above: apply concavity of $-t\log t$ to each diagonal entry expressed in a spectral decomposition, then sum the nonnegative terms. Compare the number-basis probabilities with the geometric probabilities of $\tau_{E/n}$. Summing $-p\log p\le-p\log q+q-p$ gives, when $E>0$, $$S(\rho)\le n\log(1+E/n)+h(E/n)E=ng(E/n).$$ For $E=0$, the state is the vacuum and the assertion is immediate.

Let $P_K=\mathbf1_{\{N\le K\}}$, $Q_K=I-P_K$, and $q=\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho Q_K$. The rank of $P_K$ is finite, and $q\le E/(K+1)$. Factoring off $\rho^{1/2}$ and applying the Hilbert–Schmidt Cauchy–Schwarz inequality bounds each off-diagonal block by $\sqrt q$ in trace norm. Hence $\rho$ is uniformly approximated by its finite-dimensional compression; normalizing the latter changes its trace norm by at most $q$. This proves precompactness. The energy bound is closed by lower semicontinuity of positive spectral expectations, so the set is compact.

For completeness, write $H_2(q)=-q\log q-(1-q)\log(1-q)$. Pinching into the two number blocks changes the entropy by a quantity between zero and $H_2(q)$. To see the upper bound directly, split each vector in a pure eigenensemble of $\rho$ into its two projected vectors. Concavity of $H_2$ bounds the increase of the ensemble-weight entropy by $H_2(q)$; the entropy of a pure-state mixture is at most its weight entropy. The latter fact follows by applying diagonal entropy to the Gram operator of the weighted vectors, which has the mixture’s nonzero eigenvalues. The lower bound follows from the same concavity argument for pinching.

The pinched entropy equals $$H_2(q)+(1-q)S\!\left(\frac{P_K\rho P_K}{1-q}\right)
       +qS\!\left(\frac{Q_K\rho Q_K}{q}\right),$$ where zero-mass terms are omitted. The last term is bounded by $$qn\,g\!\left(\frac{E}{qn}\right),$$ which tends to zero uniformly as $q\downarrow0$. The low-number entropy term is continuous in the finite-dimensional compression. These bounds uniformly approximate $S(\rho)$ by continuous finite-dimensional expressions and prove its continuity under the common energy bound.

Finally, normalized number compressions converge in trace norm. Their energies are at most $E/(1-q)$, and hence are uniformly bounded for all sufficiently large $K$. The established continuity proves entropy convergence. ◻

</details>

<a id="eq:thermal-relative-entropy"></a>
In particular, entropy grows only logarithmically with energy for fixed $n$, whereas a positive linear energy penalty is coercive. For $r>0$, we shall repeatedly use the identity $$\label{eq:thermal-relative-entropy}
 D(\rho\Vert\tau_r)=-S(\rho)+n\log(r+1)+h(r)\mathop{\mathrm{Tr}}\rho N.$$

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Passive mixing

For any finite collection of ports, a real orthogonal rotation of their mode operators, performed identically for every mode index, is implemented by a unitary on their joint Fock space. One way to construct it is to rotate the creation operators acting on the joint vacuum; the commutation relations show that the resulting number vectors again form an orthonormal basis. This unitary preserves combined total number. A retained subsystem therefore has energy no greater than the combined input energy. On each finite total-number sector, the unitary depends continuously on the rotation; consequently the induced channels are trace-norm continuous on every fixed state as the rotation varies.

<a id="stmt-prop-thermal-mixing"></a>
<div id="prop:thermal-mixing" class="proposition">

**Proposition 5** (Thermal mixing). *Suppose independent ports carry the $n$-mode states $\tau_{r_s}$, and the retained modes are $\sum_s\sqrt{\lambda_s}\,d_{s,j}$, where $\lambda_s\ge0$ and $\sum_s\lambda_s=1$. Their joint retained state is $$\tau_{\sum_s\lambda_s r_s}.$$*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-thermal-mixing"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-thermal-mixing</summary>

*Proof.* The coherent-state representation of a thermal state is $$\tau_r=\int_{\mathbb C^n}|z\rangle\langle z|\,
       \frac{e^{-\lVert z\rVert^2/r}}{(\pi r)^n}\,d^{2n}z.$$ Indeed, inserting the coefficients $\langle\mathbf k|z\rangle=e^{-\lVert z\rVert^2/2}z^{\mathbf k}/\sqrt{\mathbf k!}$ and integrating gives the diagonal geometric probabilities in <a href="#eq:thermal-definition" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:thermal-definition">[eq:thermal-definition]</a>. Products of coherent states transform by the same linear rotation of their amplitudes, as follows from their annihilation eigenvector equations or creation-operator expansion. Independent centered circular complex Gaussians with covariances $r_sI$ consequently produce retained covariance $(\sum_s\lambda_s r_s)I$. The displayed representation proves the claim. Zero reference means follow by continuity. ◻

</details>

This thermal representation and its relation to entropy photon number are also the starting point of the original formulation <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[GES2007](#ref-GES2007), Section II.B]. In the argument below it is only the auxiliary reference states that have this special form.

<a id="sec:interpolation"></a>
### Interpolation of diagonal form comparisons

The proof requires an interpolation principle for quadratic forms. Its hypotheses involve four simple weights. In the later application at the regularized minima, the target weight makes the squared norm of a centered thermal-balance defect equal to the normalized entropy-production pairing plus a centered-energy correction. This is the identity used in the stationarity cancellation. We prove the principle here, including the analytic property on which it depends.

<a id="int:comparison"></a>
Set <a id="int:fb"></a>$$\label{int:fb}
 f(x)=\frac{x}{\sinh x},\qquad b(x)=x\coth x,\qquad f(0)=b(0)=1.$$ Both functions are positive and even on $\mathbb R$. On Hilbert spaces $\mathcal H_j$, $0\le j\le k$, fix coordinate representations in which all weights below act by scalar multiplication. A positive weight $X_j$ defines the possibly unbounded form $$Q_{X,j}(Z)=\sum_{\alpha}X_j(\alpha)|Z_\alpha|^2;$$ fixed positive coordinate measures can be absorbed into the coordinates. Let $\mathcal T\subset\mathcal H_0$ be a linear test space, let $L_i:\mathcal T\to\mathcal H_i$ be linear, and let $w_i>0$ for $1\le i\le k$. We say that the family $X=(X_j)_{j=0}^k$ *satisfies comparison* if $$\label{int:comparison}
 Q_{X,0}(Z)\ge\sum_{i=1}^k w_i Q_{X,i}(L_iZ)
 \qquad(Z\in\mathcal T).$$ All sums on the right are interpreted as nonnegative coordinate sums.

<a id="stmt-thm-interpolation"></a>
<div id="thm:interpolation" class="theorem">

<a id="int:four-weights"></a>
**Theorem 6** (Four-weight interpolation). *At every coordinate of every space, let $m>0$ and $x,y\in\mathbb R$ be given. Suppose each $Z\in\mathcal T$ is supported on finitely many values of the parameter tuple $(m,x,y)$, and $\mathcal T$ is invariant under scalar multiplication by functions of this tuple. If the four families $$\label{int:four-weights}
 mf(x)e^x,\quad mf(x)e^{-x},\quad mf(y)e^y,\quad mf(y)e^{-y}$$ satisfy comparison, then so does <a id="int:target-weight"></a>$$\label{int:target-weight}
 F=m\frac{f(x)f(y)}{f(x+y)}.$$*

</div>

Finitely many parameter values do not mean finitely many nonzero coordinates. In particular, a centered finite matrix can have an infinite identity tail, all at the same parameter value. The hypothesis permits this situation, which will occur in our application.

<a id="int:operations"></a>
#### Operations that preserve comparison

The operations below belong to the parallel-sum and operator-mean calculus of Anderson–Duffin and Kubo–Ando <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[AndersonDuffin1969](#ref-AndersonDuffin1969), [KuboAndo1980](#ref-KuboAndo1980)]. We give direct form proofs, including the limits needed for unbounded weights.

Throughout this subsection, the auxiliary weights are positive functions of the parameter tuple $(m,x,y)$. Positive linear combinations preserve <a href="#int:comparison" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="int:comparison">[int:comparison]</a>. So do pointwise limits of such weights: on a source test there are only finitely many parameter values, so its form converges; on the target spaces Fatou’s lemma gives the required inequality.

Comparisons also persist under the *parallel sum* <a id="int:parallel"></a>$$\label{int:parallel}
 X:Y=(X^{-1}+Y^{-1})^{-1}.$$ Indeed, coordinatewise, $$(X:Y)|z|^2=\min_{u+v=z}\bigl(X|u|^2+Y|v|^2\bigr),
 \qquad
 u=\frac{Y}{X+Y}z,\quad v=\frac{X}{X+Y}z.$$ The source minimizers $U,V$ lie in $\mathcal T$. Apply the comparisons for $X$ and $Y$ to them, then use $L_iU+L_iV=L_iZ$ and the coordinatewise minimum on each target space. Target minimizers need not belong to any specified test space.

Consequently, if $X,Y$ satisfy comparison, so do their logarithmic mean $\ell(X,Y)=(X-Y)/(\log X-\log Y)$ and $XY/\ell(X,Y)$, with the continuous value $\ell(X,X)=X$. The identities that implement these operations are <a id="int:log-means"></a>$$\label{int:log-means}
 \frac1{\ell(X,Y)}=\int_0^1\frac{dt}{(1-t)X+tY},
 \qquad
 \frac{XY}{\ell(X,Y)}=\int_0^1\frac{dt}{t/X+(1-t)/Y}.$$ For the first identity, a positive quadrature followed by inversion is a finite parallel sum: if $q_j>0$ and $V_j>0$, then $$\left(\sum_{j=1}^M\frac{q_j}{V_j}\right)^{-1}
 = (V_1/q_1):\cdots:(V_M/q_M).$$ Here $V_j=(1-t_j)X+t_jY$ already satisfies comparison. The second identity is a positive average of weighted parallel sums. Pointwise limits give the integrals; zero coefficients are omitted or obtained by a limit. Thus no rule asserting preservation under multiplication of weights is being used.

<a id="int:basic-weights"></a>
Apply these two operations to each opposite-exponent pair in <a href="#int:four-weights" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="int:four-weights">[int:four-weights]</a>. Since their logarithmic mean is $m$, we obtain comparison for the three families $$\label{int:basic-weights}
 m,\qquad ms,\qquad mt,
 \qquad s=f(x)^2,\quad t=f(y)^2.$$ The next step constructs one further weight from these three.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### A Stieltjes representation

<a id="int:B-definition"></a>
<a id="int:E-integral"></a>
The function $x\mapsto f(x)^2$ decreases from $1$ to $0$ on $[0,\infty)$. Define $B$ on $(0,1]$ by $$\label{int:B-definition}
 B\bigl(f(x)^2\bigr)=b(x).$$ The scalar quadratic <a href="#int:quadratic" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="int:quadratic">[int:quadratic]</a>, used at the end of the proof, determines the candidate for the additional weight. It pairs the four original weights on $\xi\mp\phi$ and $\xi\pm\phi$ and adds $E|\phi|^2$. For a candidate $E=me>0$, write $a=b(x)+b(y)$. Its minimum is $$\frac m2\left(a-\frac{(x-y)^2}{a+2e}\right)|\xi|^2.$$ For $x^2\ne y^2$, the addition formula gives $F/m=(yb(x)+xb(y))/(x+y)$. Equating the minimum with $F|\xi|^2$ therefore requires, within this quadratic construction, $$(a+2e)(b(x)-b(y))=x^2-y^2,
 \qquad
 E=\frac{m(t-s)}{2(B(s)-B(t))},
 \quad s=f(x)^2,\quad t=f(y)^2,$$ where the last equality uses $b(z)^2=f(z)^2+z^2$. The final minimization will verify the value in all cases. It remains to obtain comparison for this candidate from the known weights. Its reciprocal has the integral form, initially for $0<s,t<1$, $$\label{int:E-integral}
 \frac1E=\frac2m\int_0^1
       -B'\bigl(\theta s+(1-\theta)t\bigr)\,d\theta,
 \qquad s=f(x)^2,\quad t=f(y)^2.$$ The lemma below will show that this defines a positive continuous weight also when $s=1$ or $t=1$. A Stieltjes representation of $-B'$ turns the reciprocal into a limit of positive combinations of $1/m$ and reciprocals of $\theta(ms)+(1-\theta)(mt)+rm$, with $r\ge0$. The parallel-sum argument above will then construct $E$ from the three known weights. We now prove the required representation.

<a id="stmt-int-stieltjes"></a>
<div id="int:stieltjes" class="lemma">

<a id="int:stieltjes-form"></a>
**Lemma 7**. *The function $B$ extends holomorphically to the upper half-plane and across the positive real axis, is real and strictly decreasing there, and satisfies $B(1)=1$, $B'(1)=-1$. There are a constant $c\ge0$ and a positive measure $\nu$ on $[0,\infty)$ such that $$\label{int:stieltjes-form}
 -B'(s)=c+\int_{[0,\infty)}\frac{d\nu(r)}{s+r}
 \quad(s>0),\qquad
